Also in [30], the notion of canonical unramified wavefront set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^K\!{\underline{\textsf{WF}}}(\pi )$$\end{document}KWF_(π) was introduced. This is a collection of classes under a certain equivalence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^K\textsf{WF}(\pi )$$\end{document}KWF(π). The invariant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^K\!{\underline{\textsf{WF}}}(\pi )$$\end{document}KWF_(π) is a set of pairs\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ({\mathcal {O}},{\bar{C}}):\ {\mathcal {O}}\text { nilpotent} G \text {-orbit in }{\mathfrak {g}},\ {\bar{C}}\text { conjugacy class in }{\bar{A}}({\mathcal {O}}), $$\end{document}(O,C¯):OnilpotentG-orbit ing,C¯conjugacy class inA¯(O),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\bar{A}}({\mathcal {O}})$$\end{document}A¯(O) is Lusztig’s canonical quotient. The duality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_S$$\end{document}dS factors through the canonical quotient, so we may talk about \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_S({}^K\!{\underline{\textsf{WF}}}(\pi ))$$\end{document}dS(KWF_(π)).

Conjectures

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )$$\end{document}k¯WF(π) denote the geometric wavefront set of an admissible representation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}^\omega ({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}Gω(k). This is a collection of adjoint nilpotent orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {g}}({\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}})$$\end{document}g(k¯). The nilpotent orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {g}}({\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}})$$\end{document}g(k¯) are ordered with respect to the partial order given by the Zariski closure. The nilpotent orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )$$\end{document}k¯WF(π) are by definition non-comparable in the closure order. In almost all known examples, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )$$\end{document}k¯WF(π) consists of a single nilpotent orbit if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is irreducible, but not always, as recent examples of Tsai [33] have shown.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {S}}_1,{\mathcal {S}}_2$$\end{document}S1,S2 are two sets of nilpotent orbits, we write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\mathcal {S}}_1\le {\mathcal {S}}_2, $$\end{document}S1≤S2,if for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_1\in {\mathcal {S}}_1$$\end{document}O1∈S1, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_2\in {\mathcal {S}}_2$$\end{document}O2∈S2 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_1\le {\mathcal {O}}_2$$\end{document}O1≤O2 in the closure order.

Conjecture 1.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee ,{\mathcal {L}}^\vee )$$\end{document}π(λ,s,O∨,L∨) be an irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}^\omega ({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}Gω(k)-representation in the L-packet \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi (\varphi )=\Pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee )$$\end{document}Π(φ)=Π(λ,s,O∨). Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee ).$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(G∨·O∨). Equivalently,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee \subset \overline{d({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )))}$$\end{document}O∨⊂d(k¯WF(AZ(π)))¯.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee ).$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(G∨·O∨). Equivalently,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee ).$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(G∨·O∨). Equivalently,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee \subset \overline{d({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )))}$$\end{document}O∨⊂d(k¯WF(AZ(π)))¯.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee \subset \overline{d({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )))}$$\end{document}O∨⊂d(k¯WF(AZ(π)))¯.

Of course, the conjecture is contingent on the existence of a local Langlands correspondence.

Remark 1.2

While this paper was being finalized, we learned that Conjecture 1.1, as well as the connection with Conjecture 1.9 and Jiang’s conjecture, have also been formulated independently and studied for classical groups by Hazeltine et al. [15]. In loc. cit., the conjectures are stated and investigated for general groups (rather than ‘inner to split’), see [15, Conjecture 1.1, Remarks 1.9, 7.11].

In terms of the discussion around (1.3), the conjecture could be rephrased as1.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )\le d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}^\vee _\textsf{FT})=d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}^\vee _{{\textsf{AZ}}}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}k¯WF(π)≤d∨(OFT∨)=d∨(OAZ∨),whenever we know (or expect) that the action of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ on the space of geometric parameter is given by the geometric Fourier transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\operatorname {FT}$$\end{document}FT.

Remark 1.3

The conjecture aims to generalize the recent results for Lusztig unipotent representations (trivial \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi (I_{{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}φ(Ik)) with real infinitesimal character, see [7, 8], and for unipotent supercuspidal representations [9]. In these cases, the upper bound is an equality.

But even in the case of unipotent representations there is an example for the split group \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_7$$\end{document}E7 [8, §1] that shows that for unipotent representations with nonreal infinitesimal character, the upper bound can be strict. At the end of Sect. 5, we will comment on other possible versions of the conjecture involving the duality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_S$$\end{document}dS and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^K\!{\underline{\textsf{WF}}}(\pi )$$\end{document}KWF_(π).

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{GL}}(n)$$\end{document}GL(n), Conjecture 1.1 is true, in fact a stronger statement holds.

Theorem 1.4

(Mœglin-Waldspurger [28, II.2]) For every irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{GL}}(n,{{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}GL(n,k)-representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π)) is a singleton and1.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))=\{d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}_\pi ^\vee )\}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))={d∨(Oπ∨)},where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}_\pi ^\vee $$\end{document}Oπ∨ is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^\vee $$\end{document}G∨-saturation of the nilpotent Langlands parameter of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π.

To exemplify Conjecture 1.1, we will verify it for the depth-zero supercuspidal representations of classical groups, using the Langlands parametrization of Lust and Stevens [24], and for the irreducible representations of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_2$$\end{document}G2, using Aubert and Xu [3] and Gan and Savin [14, 15].

Remark 1.5

If an irreducible smooth representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is generic, then its geometric wavefront set is the principal orbit. For the conjecture to hold, we would need \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {O}}^\vee _{\textsf{AZ}}=0$$\end{document}OAZ∨=0, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee $$\end{document}O∨ would need to be the open dense orbit in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q$$\end{document}hq∨. Moreover, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {L}}^\vee $$\end{document}L∨ would have to be the trivial local system.

In the case of supercuspidal representations, Conjecture 1.1 takes a simpler, more concrete form. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is supercuspidal, the Langlands parameter is expected to satisfy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee ={\mathcal {O}}_{\lambda ,s}^\vee $$\end{document}O∨=Oλ,s∨, the unique open dense \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^\vee (s)$$\end{document}H∨(s)-orbit in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q$$\end{document}hq∨ (this is in fact the case whenever the representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is tempered). In addition,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )=\pi $$\end{document}AZ(π)=π (immediate from the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ).

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee ={\mathcal {O}}_{\lambda ,s}^\vee $$\end{document}O∨=Oλ,s∨, the unique open dense \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^\vee (s)$$\end{document}H∨(s)-orbit in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q$$\end{document}hq∨ (this is in fact the case whenever the representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is tempered). In addition,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee ={\mathcal {O}}_{\lambda ,s}^\vee $$\end{document}O∨=Oλ,s∨, the unique open dense \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^\vee (s)$$\end{document}H∨(s)-orbit in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q$$\end{document}hq∨ (this is in fact the case whenever the representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is tempered). In addition,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )=\pi $$\end{document}AZ(π)=π (immediate from the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ).

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )=\pi $$\end{document}AZ(π)=π (immediate from the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ).

Conjecture 1.6

(Supercuspidals) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s},{\mathcal {L}}^\vee )$$\end{document}π(λ,s,Oλ,s∨,L∨) be an irreducible supercuspidal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}^\omega ({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}Gω(k)-representation in the L-packet \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi (\varphi )=\Pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s})$$\end{document}Π(φ)=Π(λ,s,Oλ,s∨). Set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }=G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s}$$\end{document}Oφ∨=G∨·Oλ,s∨. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )\le d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }). $$\end{document}k¯WF(π)≤d∨(Oφ∨). Equivalently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }\le d({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi ))$$\end{document}Oφ∨≤d(k¯WF(π)).If in addition, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}^\omega ({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}Gω(k) is split and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is depth-zero compactly induced from a cuspidal representation of the maximal hyperspecial parahoric subgroup, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )=\{d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi })\}. $$\end{document}k¯WF(π)={d∨(Oφ∨)}.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )\le d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }). $$\end{document}k¯WF(π)≤d∨(Oφ∨). Equivalently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }\le d({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi ))$$\end{document}Oφ∨≤d(k¯WF(π)).

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )\le d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }). $$\end{document}k¯WF(π)≤d∨(Oφ∨). Equivalently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi }\le d({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi ))$$\end{document}Oφ∨≤d(k¯WF(π)).

If in addition, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}^\omega ({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}Gω(k) is split and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is depth-zero compactly induced from a cuspidal representation of the maximal hyperspecial parahoric subgroup, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )=\{d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi })\}. $$\end{document}k¯WF(π)={d∨(Oφ∨)}.

If in addition, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}^\omega ({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}Gω(k) is split and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is depth-zero compactly induced from a cuspidal representation of the maximal hyperspecial parahoric subgroup, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )=\{d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{\varphi })\}. $$\end{document}k¯WF(π)={d∨(Oφ∨)}.

The first part of the conjecture is just a particular case of Conjecture 1.1.

Remark 1.7

Notice that Conjecture 1.6 is vacuously true for the nonsingular supercuspidal representations, in the sense of Kaletha [18], because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _\varphi =0$$\end{document}Oφ∨=0 so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _\varphi )$$\end{document}d∨(Oφ∨) is the principal geometric orbit. In this case, the upper bound is attained only for the generic constituents of the L-packets. In Sect. 3.1, we will compute the wavefront sets of the regular depth-zero supercuspidals parameterized by Kazhdan and Varshavsky [22] and DeBacker and Reeder [11].

In Sect. 4, we will prove

Theorem 1.8

(Depth-zero supercuspidals of classical groups) Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}G(k) is a symplectic, special orthogonal, or unitary group. Conjecture 1.6 holds for all depth-zero supercuspidal representations, using the Langlands parametrization of Lust and Stevens [24].

We mention that Conjectures 1.1 and 1.6 are compatible with a conjecture about Arthur packets formulated by D. Jiang. Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi $$\end{document}φ is a tempered L-parameter. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\Pi (\varphi ))$$\end{document}AZ(Π(φ)) is expected to be an Arthur packet. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi '\in {\textsf{AZ}}(\Pi (\varphi ))$$\end{document}π′∈AZ(Π(φ)) be one of these “anti-tempered” representations. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi '={\textsf{AZ}}(\pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s},{\mathcal {L}}^\vee ))$$\end{document}π′=AZ(π(λ,s,Oλ,s∨,L∨)) for some local system \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {L}}^\vee $$\end{document}L∨. Conjecture 1.1 then says that we should have1.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi ')\le d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}^\vee _\varphi ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}k¯WF(π′)≤d∨(Oφ∨).The orbit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\varphi }$$\end{document}Oφ∨ is precisely the “Arthur SL(2)” \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{A,\varphi }$$\end{document}OA,φ∨ attached to the Arthur packet \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\Pi (\varphi ))$$\end{document}AZ(Π(φ)). In other words, the expectation is1.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi ')\le d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _{A,\varphi }), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}k¯WF(π′)≤d∨(OA,φ∨),for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi '\in {\textsf{AZ}}(\Pi (\varphi ))$$\end{document}π′∈AZ(Π(φ)). This is known to hold (with equality) if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi $$\end{document}φ is a “real” unramified L-parameter, see [10]. Jiang’s conjecture [16, Conjecture 4.2] says that this inequality holds for all Arthur packets, when they are defined (not just the above anti-tempered packets) and moreover that there exists at least one constituent of the Arthur packet where the equality is achieved. One can naturally reformulate Conjecture 1.1 in this case as follows:

Conjecture 1.9

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi =\pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s},{\mathcal {L}}^\vee )$$\end{document}π=π(λ,s,Oλ,s∨,L∨) be an irreducible representation in the tempered L-packet \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi (\varphi )=\Pi (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s})$$\end{document}Π(φ)=Π(λ,s,Oλ,s∨). Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}^\vee _\varphi ).$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(Oφ∨).Equality in (1) is achieved for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π in the L-packet.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}^\vee _\varphi ).$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(Oφ∨).

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee ({\mathbb {O}}^\vee _\varphi ).$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(Oφ∨).

Equality in (1) is achieved for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π in the L-packet.

Equality in (1) is achieved for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π in the L-packet.

In Sect. 2, we will explain that Conjecture 1.9(1) (via the usual desiderata of Langlands correspondence) implies Conjecture 1.1.

In Sect. 5, we will verify Conjecture 1.9 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_2$$\end{document}G2, using the Langlands correspondences from [3, 14, 15] and the wavefront set results of [17, 23]:

Theorem 1.10

Conjectures 1.1 and 1.9 hold for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_2$$\end{document}G2.

Remark 1.11

We look at the two extreme cases of Conjectures 1.1 and 1.9. Firstly, let us call an irreducible representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π anti-generic if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )$$\end{document}AZ(π) is generic. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is anti-generic, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))={\mathcal {O}}_{reg }$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))=Oreg, the regular nilpotent orbit. The only way that the upper bound conjecture could hold then is if the Langlands nilpotent parameter of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is 0. In other words, for every anti-generic representation the image of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {C}}\subset W_{{\textsf{k}}}'$$\end{document}C⊂Wk′ under the Langlands parameter is trivial. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is unipotent anti-generic, then it is known that it is spherical (with respect to the maximal special parahoric) and the desired condition on the Langlands parameter holds. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is supercuspidal, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )=\pi $$\end{document}AZ(π)=π, the conjecture implies that every generic supercuspidal representation has trivial nilpotent Langlands parameter. For classical groups, the algorithm for computing the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ-dual in [1] implies that the Langlands nilpotent parameter of an anti-generic representation is indeed trivial.Secondly, suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee =0$$\end{document}O∨=0, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee )={\mathcal {O}}_{reg }.$$\end{document}d∨(G∨·O∨)=Oreg. Then Conjecture 1.9(1) is trivially satisfied, but (2) says that there should exist a representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π in the tempered L-packet such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))={\mathcal {O}}_{reg }$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))=Oreg. In other words, every tempered L-packet with trivial Langlands nilpotent parameter should contain an anti-generic representation. This is equivalent with a conjecture of Shahidi as follows. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \leftrightarrow (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s})$$\end{document}φ↔(λ,s,Oλ,s∨) is a tempered Langlands parameter, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s}$$\end{document}Oλ,s∨ must be dense in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q$$\end{document}hq∨, we can only have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s}=0$$\end{document}Oλ,s∨=0 if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q=0$$\end{document}hq∨=0. But then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi $$\end{document}φ is the only L-parameter for the pair \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\lambda ,s)$$\end{document}(λ,s) and since it is expected that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ preserves the part \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\lambda ,s)$$\end{document}(λ,s) of the Langlands parameter, it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\Pi (\varphi ))=\Pi (\varphi ). $$\end{document}AZ(Π(φ))=Π(φ).

Firstly, let us call an irreducible representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π anti-generic if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )$$\end{document}AZ(π) is generic. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is anti-generic, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))={\mathcal {O}}_{reg }$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))=Oreg, the regular nilpotent orbit. The only way that the upper bound conjecture could hold then is if the Langlands nilpotent parameter of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is 0. In other words, for every anti-generic representation the image of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {C}}\subset W_{{\textsf{k}}}'$$\end{document}C⊂Wk′ under the Langlands parameter is trivial. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is unipotent anti-generic, then it is known that it is spherical (with respect to the maximal special parahoric) and the desired condition on the Langlands parameter holds. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is supercuspidal, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )=\pi $$\end{document}AZ(π)=π, the conjecture implies that every generic supercuspidal representation has trivial nilpotent Langlands parameter. For classical groups, the algorithm for computing the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ-dual in [1] implies that the Langlands nilpotent parameter of an anti-generic representation is indeed trivial.

Firstly, let us call an irreducible representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π anti-generic if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )$$\end{document}AZ(π) is generic. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is anti-generic, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))={\mathcal {O}}_{reg }$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))=Oreg, the regular nilpotent orbit. The only way that the upper bound conjecture could hold then is if the Langlands nilpotent parameter of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is 0. In other words, for every anti-generic representation the image of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {C}}\subset W_{{\textsf{k}}}'$$\end{document}C⊂Wk′ under the Langlands parameter is trivial. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is unipotent anti-generic, then it is known that it is spherical (with respect to the maximal special parahoric) and the desired condition on the Langlands parameter holds. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is supercuspidal, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )=\pi $$\end{document}AZ(π)=π, the conjecture implies that every generic supercuspidal representation has trivial nilpotent Langlands parameter. Secondly, suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee =0$$\end{document}O∨=0, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee )={\mathcal {O}}_{reg }.$$\end{document}d∨(G∨·O∨)=Oreg. In other words, every tempered L-packet with trivial Langlands nilpotent parameter should contain an anti-generic representation. This is equivalent with a conjecture of Shahidi as follows. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \leftrightarrow (\lambda ,s,{\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s})$$\end{document}φ↔(λ,s,Oλ,s∨) is a tempered Langlands parameter, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s}$$\end{document}Oλ,s∨ must be dense in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q$$\end{document}hq∨, we can only have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee _{\lambda ,s}=0$$\end{document}Oλ,s∨=0 if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathfrak {h}}}^\vee _q=0$$\end{document}hq∨=0. Parabolic induction

In this section, we discuss the compatibility of our conjectures with parabolic induction. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf{P}}={\textbf{M}}{\textbf{N}}$$\end{document}P=MN be a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{k}}$$\end{document}k-rational parabolic subgroup of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf{G}}}$$\end{document}G with a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{k}}$$\end{document}k-rational Levi subgroup \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M={\textbf{M}}({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}M=M(k).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi =i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{{\textbf{G}}}({{\textsf{k}}})}(\pi _M)$$\end{document}π=iP(k)G(k)(πM) is a parabolically induced representation, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi _M$$\end{document}πM is an admissible representation of M, then the relation between the wavefront sets is well known, see Waldspurger [35]:2.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi )=\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{{\textbf{G}}}}({}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(\pi _M)), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}k¯WF(π)=indMG(k¯WF(πM)),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{{\textbf{G}}}}$$\end{document}indMG denotes the Lusztig–Spaltenstein induction of nilpotent orbits.

We need to use several known facts about the induction of nilpotent orbits.

Lemma 2.1

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_1\le {\mathcal {O}}_2$$\end{document}O1≤O2 are two nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf{M}}$$\end{document}M-orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {m}}$$\end{document}m, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}({\mathcal {O}}_1)\le \textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}({\mathcal {O}}_2).$$\end{document}indMG(O1)≤indMG(O2).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_1\le {\mathcal {O}}_2$$\end{document}O1≤O2 are two nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf{M}}$$\end{document}M-orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {m}}$$\end{document}m, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}({\mathcal {O}}_1)\le \textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}({\mathcal {O}}_2).$$\end{document}indMG(O1)≤indMG(O2).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_1^\vee \le {\mathcal {O}}_2^\vee $$\end{document}O1∨≤O2∨ are two nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^\vee $$\end{document}G∨-orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {g}}^\vee $$\end{document}g∨, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee ({\mathcal {O}}_2^\vee )\le d^\vee ({\mathcal {O}}_1^\vee )$$\end{document}d∨(O2∨)≤d∨(O1∨).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}_1^\vee \le {\mathcal {O}}_2^\vee $$\end{document}O1∨≤O2∨ are two nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^\vee $$\end{document}G∨-orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {g}}^\vee $$\end{document}g∨, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee ({\mathcal {O}}_2^\vee )\le d^\vee ({\mathcal {O}}_1^\vee )$$\end{document}d∨(O2∨)≤d∨(O1∨).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee $$\end{document}O∨ is a nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^\vee $$\end{document}M∨-orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {m}}^\vee $$\end{document}m∨, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee )=\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}(d^\vee ({\mathcal {O}}^\vee )).$$\end{document}d∨(G∨·O∨)=indMG(d∨(O∨)).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {O}}^\vee $$\end{document}O∨ is a nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^\vee $$\end{document}M∨-orbits in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathfrak {m}}^\vee $$\end{document}m∨, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d^\vee (G^\vee \cdot {\mathcal {O}}^\vee )=\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}(d^\vee ({\mathcal {O}}^\vee )).$$\end{document}d∨(G∨·O∨)=indMG(d∨(O∨)).

Proof

Claim (1) is immediate from the definition, or for example from the formula \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{\textbf{G}}}({\mathcal {O}})}={\textbf{G}}\cdot (\overline{{\mathcal {O}}}+{\mathfrak {n}})$$\end{document}indMG(O)¯=G·(O¯+n), see [6, proof of Theorem 7.1.3]. Claims (2) and (3) are in [4, Proposition A2 (a),(c)]. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proposition 2.2

Conjecture 1.9(1) implies Conjecture 1.1.

Proof

We need to prove that if\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le d^\vee ({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _\pi ) $$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤d∨(Oπ∨)holds for all irreducible tempered representations of reductive groups, then it holds for all irreducible representations. Here \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _\pi $$\end{document}Oπ∨ denotes the nilpotent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^\vee $$\end{document}G∨-orbit of the Langlands parameter of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π. By the parabolic part of the Langlands classification, every irreducible \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi $$\end{document}π is a quotient of a standard module \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i({\textbf{P}},\sigma _\nu )=i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{\textbf{G}}({{\textsf{k}}})}(\sigma _\nu )$$\end{document}i(P,σν)=iP(k)G(k)(σν), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma _\nu =\sigma \otimes \nu $$\end{document}σν=σ⊗ν, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma $$\end{document}σ is an irreducible tempered \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf{M}}({{\textsf{k}}})$$\end{document}M(k).

Since the involution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}$$\end{document}AZ commutes with parabolic induction [2, Theorem 1.7], i.e., in the Grothendieck group\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\textsf{AZ}}(i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{\textbf{G}}({{\textsf{k}}})}(\sigma _\nu ))=i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{\textbf{G}}({{\textsf{k}}})}({\textsf{AZ}}(\sigma )_\nu ), $$\end{document}AZ(iP(k)G(k)(σν))=iP(k)G(k)(AZ(σ)ν),it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textsf{AZ}}(\pi )$$\end{document}AZ(π) is a subquotient of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{\textbf{G}}({{\textsf{k}}})}({\textsf{AZ}}(\sigma )_\nu )$$\end{document}iP(k)G(k)(AZ(σ)ν). By the definition of the wavefront set then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{\textbf{G}}({{\textsf{k}}})}({\textsf{AZ}}(\sigma )_\nu )= ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}(i_{{\textbf{P}}({{\textsf{k}}})}^{{\textbf{G}}({{\textsf{k}}})}({\textsf{AZ}}(\sigma ))), $$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤k¯WF(iP(k)G(k)(AZ(σ)ν)=k¯WF(iP(k)G(k)(AZ(σ))),where the equality comes from the fact that the local character expansion is valid on neighborhoods of compact elements, and so it does not see twists by unramified characters. By (2.1), it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\pi ))\le \textsf{ind}_{{\textbf{M}}}^{{{\textbf{G}}}}( ^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\sigma ))). $$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(π))≤indMG(k¯WF(AZ(σ))).Now assuming Conjecture 1.9 for tempered representations, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${}^{{\bar{{{\textsf{k}}}}}}\textsf{WF}({\textsf{AZ}}(\sigma ))\le d^\vee _{M^\vee }({{\mathbb {O}}}^\vee _\sigma )$$\end{document}k¯WF(AZ(σ))≤d∨∨(Oσ∨). Theorem 3.1

([9, Theorem 3.0.4]) The canonical unramified wavefront set of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi (x,{{\widetilde{\sigma }}})$$\end{document}π(x,σ~) is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ ^K\!{\underline{\textsf{WF}}}(\pi (x,{{\widetilde{\sigma }}}))=(\textsf{WF}(\sigma ), x). $$\end{document}KWF_(π(x,σ~))=(WF(σ),x).

Lusztig gave an explicit description of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textsf{WF}(\mu )$$\end{document}WF(μ), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ an irreducible representation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {G}^\omega _x$$\end{document}Gxω in terms of his parametrization [25, Theorem 4.23]. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {G}^\omega _x)^\vee $$\end{document}(Gxω)∨ be the dual finite reductive group. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\tau , u_0^\vee ,y)$$\end{document}(τ,u0∨,y), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_0^\vee $$\end{document}u0∨. The element y will not be important for us.
